As shown in table
8
, the values of the fluxes for these solutions can be expressed in terms of two parameters, one of them being precisely the value of the scalar field
\mathcal{A}
. Indeed, they can all be interpreted as a generalization of the 1-dimensional solutions discussed in
[
23
]
and
[
28
]
in a closed string scenario. Those solutions can be restored if we take the limit
\mathcal{A}\,\to\,0
, with the condition that
\frac{\lambda}{\mathcal{A}}
remains constant: in this way, the
\mathcal{A}^{2}
correction to the flux
a_{0}
disappears and the flux
\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
becomes independent of the closed string sector. In other words, we come back to a solution in the origin, with an open string flux
\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{K}}}_{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
that, even if not vanishing, does never contribute to the scalar potential, as already explained.

The vacuum energy densities associated to these solutions are

V_{0}(\mathbf{1})=-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{10}\quad,\quad V_{0}(\mathbf{2})=-\frac{5\lambda^{2}}{48}\quad,\quad V_{0}(\mathbf{3})=-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{12}\quad,\quad V_{0}(\mathbf{4})=-\frac{3\lambda^{2}}{16}\quad,

(141)

which coincide, up to a proper rescaling of the parameter
\lambda
employed, with the values for the old solutions in the closed string sector.

Looking for further new solutions possibly out of the origin, we focused in particular on the possibility of vacua with no Roman’s mass, namely
a_{3}=0
. However, this investigation led us to the conclusion that - besides vacua with all open string fluxes vanishing - the only such vacua are Minkowski vacua in the origin with fluxes

b_{1}=a_{2}\;,\qquad a_{0}=a_{1}=a_{3}=b_{0}=c_{0}=c_{1}=\bar{c}_{1}=0\;,\qquad g_{0}=0\;,

(142)

for any value of the flux
g_{1}
. Taking into account the fact that
g_{1}
is not coupled to the other fluxes and does not contribute to the scalar potential, these vacua correspond to a 1-dimensional subset of the 2-dimensional critical points described in
[
23
]
in a purely closed string setting, dual to GKP ones
[
38
]
.

As far as residual supersymmetry is concerned, solving the eigenvalues equation of
\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
, we find that only the solutions of family
\mathbf{1}
with
s_{2}=+
preserve
\mathcal{N}=1
supersymmetry, having
\kappa_{1}=\sqrt{-3V}
, while the remaining ones are non-supersymmetric. We can have a double check of this result by diagonalizing the fermionic shift matrix
\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
: only one zero eigenvalue appears in the case of solutions of family
\mathbf{1}
with
s_{2}=+
, while all the other solutions give a matrix
\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
with non-vanishing eigenvalues.

In order to understand the stability properties of each solution, we would like to compute the associated mass spectra for the complete set of scalar fields. To this purpose, we can still follow the procedure developed in
[
37
]
, but only after properly rotating the embedding tensor. Since the procedure is only valid at the origin, we have to find the rotation that brings a generic point of the moduli space with non-vanishing
\mathcal{A}
back to the origin and apply the inverse rotation to the embedding tensor indices.

Tables
9
and
10
collect the results obtained for the normalized masses. In each table, we colour the multiplicities associated to the masses coming from the open string sector; in particular, the masses that are common to any choice of the signs are shown in blue, while the values that depend on the sign
s_{2}
are highlighted in red.

We can deduce that the stable extrema of the scalar potential, i.e. the solutions where tachyons are not present or satisfy the B.F. bound
m^{2}\geq-\frac{3}{4}
, are

•

solutions of family
\mathbf{1}
for any choice of the signs;

•

solutions of family
\mathbf{3}
for any choice of the signs;

•

solutions of family
\mathbf{4}
only for
s_{2}=+
.

In the remaining cases, there is a tachyon exceeding the B.F. bound, which is highlighted in gray in tables
9
and
10
.

5.3
The case
\mathfrak{N}=6
at the origin

We have also explored the case
\mathfrak{N}=6
at the origin and have found some solutions beyond the ones that can be trivially extended from the configurations found for
\mathfrak{N}=3
.

Having
\mathfrak{N}=6
allows us to consider, at least, 2 nonvanishing values for the structure constants (modulo their antisymmetric components). This way, we can solve the quadratic constraint (
42
) by considering gauge fluxes
{\cal F}^{I}{}_{ab}
along the orthogonal components for which the structure constant are nonzero.

By splitting the indices
I
as
I=\{I^{\prime},\,I^{\prime\prime}\}
(
I^{\prime},I^{\prime\prime}=1,2,3
) and for consistency with the SO(3) truncation, we consider the following fluxes:

\displaystyle{\cal F}^{I^{\prime}}{}_{ab}=

\displaystyle\ \varepsilon_{abc}\delta^{cI^{\prime}}g_{0}\ ,

\displaystyle g_{I^{\prime}J^{\prime}}{}^{K^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\ \varepsilon_{I^{\prime}J^{\prime}L^{\prime}}\delta^{L^{\prime}K^{\prime}}g_{1}\ ,

(143)

\displaystyle{\cal F}^{I^{\prime\prime}}{}_{ab}=

\displaystyle\ \varepsilon_{abc}\delta^{cI^{\prime\prime}}g_{0}^{\prime}\ ,

\displaystyle g_{I^{\prime\prime}J^{\prime\prime}}{}^{K^{\prime\prime}}=

\displaystyle\ \varepsilon_{I^{\prime\prime}J^{\prime\prime}L^{\prime\prime}}\delta^{L^{\prime\prime}K^{\prime\prime}}g_{1}^{\prime}\ ,

(144)

which correspond to
G_{\text{YM}}=\text{SO}(3)\times\text{SO}(3)
.

Upon focusing on non-Minkowskian vacua and imposing the coexistence of both fluxes
{\cal F}^{I}{}_{ab}
and
g_{IJ}{}^{K}
via the constraints

\displaystyle g_{0}\,g_{1}^{\prime}\neq 0\ \qquad\text{or}\qquad g_{1}\,g_{0}^{\prime}\neq 0\ ,

(145)

we obtain a set of 1-parameter solutions, which are shown in Table
11
.

Solutions

a_{0}

a_{3}

b_{1}

c_{1}=\bar{c}_{1}

g_{1}

g^{\prime}_{1}

g_{0}

g^{\prime}_{0}

The value of the potential at the origin for each family of solutions is:

\displaystyle V(\mathbb{1}_{(s_{1},s_{2})})

\displaystyle=V(\mathbb{1^{\prime}}_{(s_{1},s_{2})})=-\frac{3}{32}\left(9-\sqrt{65}\right)\lambda^{2}\ <\ 0\ ,

(146)

\displaystyle V(\mathbb{2}_{(s_{1},s_{2})})

\displaystyle=V(\mathbb{2^{\prime}}_{(s_{1},s_{2})})=-\frac{3}{32}\left(9+\sqrt{65}\right)\lambda^{2}\ <\ 0\ ,

(147)

thus yielding AdS solutions in all cases. However, the minimum of the mass spectrum for
\mathbb{1}_{(s_{1},s_{2})}
and
\mathbb{1^{\prime}}_{(s_{1},s_{2})}
is
m^{2}=-0,9997
, and for
\mathbb{2}_{(s_{1},s_{2})}
and
\mathbb{2^{\prime}}_{(s_{1},s_{2})}
is
m^{2}=-1.3150
. So, none of them satisfy the B.F. bound, and therefore these solutions are unstable.

6
Flux quantization and perturbative control

It is well-known that 10D supergravity is a good low-energy effective description for strings in the limit where both the higher-loop expansion governed by the string coupling
g_{s}
and the higher-derivative expansion governed by
\alpha^{\prime}
are under control. In practice this means to adopt a compactification setup where the internal volume is large w.r.t. the string scale and
e^{\Phi}
is small.

Now, within the 4D supergravity description, if we restrict to the metric and the scalars, the action may be cast in the following form

S_{\textrm{4D}}\,=\,\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g_{4}}\,\left(\frac{M_{\textrm{Pl}}^{2}}{2}\left(\mathcal{R}_{4}-\frac{1}{2}K_{AB}\,\partial\phi^{A}\partial\phi^{B}\right)-M_{\textrm{Pl}}^{4}\,V(\phi)\right)\,+\,\dots\ ,

(148)

where
K_{AB}
is the metric on the scalar manifold and
M_{\textrm{Pl}}
is the 4D Planck scale. Note that, when expressing things in this way all the fields and embedding tensor deformations appearing in the 4D action are
dimensionless
. This means that, in order to make our 10D/4D dictionary dimensionally correct, we need to reinsert the right powers of
(2\pi\ell_{s})
everywhere in our reduction
Ansatz
. This will read

ds_{10}^{2}\,=\,\tau^{-2}ds_{4}^{2}\,+\,\left(2\pi\ell_{s}\right)^{2}\rho\left(\sigma^{2}ds_{3,a}^{2}\,+\,\sigma^{-2}ds_{3,i}^{2}\right)\ ,

(149)

where
\rho
,
\tau
and
\sigma
are dimensionless. In particular,
\rho
measures the internal volume in string units. In these units, the integrated fluxes need to be quantized according to

\oint\limits_{\mathcal{C}_{3}}H_{(3)}\,\in\,\left(2\pi\ell_{s}\right)^{2}\mathbb{Z}\ ,\qquad\oint\limits_{\mathcal{C}_{p}}F_{(p)}\,\in\,\left(2\pi\ell_{s}\right)^{p-1}\mathbb{Z}\ ,\qquad\omega_{mn}{}^{p}\,\in\,\mathbb{Z}\ .

(150)

In this way, the objects appearing in our dictionary in table
4
are directly to be identified with the integers appearing above. For what concerns the open string flux, this obeys

\oint\limits_{\mathcal{C}_{2}}\mathcal{F}\,\in\,2\pi\mathbb{Z}\ .

(151)

The vacua presented here are all at
\rho=1
and with unquantized fluxes. However, we will show in this section that, we can act with a 4D rescaling that transforms both the scalars and the fluxes into a configuration with physically desirable features. The rescaling we need is a combination

\mathbb{R}^{+}_{\Omega}\,\equiv\,\mathbb{R}^{+}_{\textrm{trombone}}\,\times\,\mathbb{R}^{+}_{\rho}\,\times\,\mathbb{R}^{+}_{\tau}\ ,

(152)

where the trombone
\mathbb{R}^{+}
rescales the whole 4D Lagrangian leaving the EOM’s invariant.

\begin{array}[]{lcclcclc}\rho\ \sim\ \Omega^{2}&,&&\tau\ \sim\ \Omega^{6}&,&&\sigma\ \sim\ \Omega^{0}&,\end{array}

(153)

where
\Omega\,\in\,\mathbb{R}^{+}
will be chosen to be parametrically large. Note that we do not want
\sigma
to scale non-trivially in
\Omega
, as it appears in the 10D metric both with positive and negative powers, hence we would necessarily have some internal cycle dropping below the string scale when
\Omega
is chosen large.
In table
12
we display the scaling behavior of all the fluxes considered in our solutions.

Fluxes

H_{(3)}

F_{(p)}

\omega

\mathcal{F}^{I}

\Omega
weights

\Omega^{2}

\Omega^{2+p}

\Omega^{0}

\Omega^{4}

Table 12
:
The scaling weights of all the fluxes that appear as ingredients in our massive type IIA compactification setup.

It may be worth mentioning that all the flux numbers that grow large in the large
\Omega
regime, become insensitive to the flux quantization conditions.
Note that
\mathcal{F}^{I}
contains both the pure YM flux part and its non-Abelian field strength contribution expressed in terms of the structure constants
g_{IJ}{}^{K}
, which need to scale as
\Omega^{-4}
if
\mathcal{A}^{I}
is assumed to scale like
\mathcal{F}^{I}
. This in turn implies that the gauge generators
t_{I}
scale as
\Omega^{-4}
. In this way the full non-Abelian field strength
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{I}t_{I}
is invariant under
\Omega
.

The physical scales controlling the reliability of our setup are analyzed in table
13
, in terms of their parametric behavior in
\Omega
. As it may be seen in the table, all our AdS vacuum solutions presented in this work appear to be reliable, whether or not they preserve supersymmetry. However, they cannot be regarded as proper 4D vacua, in the sense that they fail to realize scale separation. As noted in
[
39
]
, this is generically expected in 10D solutions with non-zero internal curvature.

Scales

g_{s}

\frac{Vol_{6}}{\left(2\pi\ell_{s}\right)^{6}}

\frac{|\Lambda|}{M_{\textrm{Pl}}^{4}}

\frac{\ell_{\textrm{KK}}}{\ell_{\textrm{AdS}}}

\Omega
weights

\Omega^{-3}

\Omega^{6}

\Omega^{-14}

\Omega^{0}

Table 13
:
The scaling weights of the physical scales in these compactifications. It is apparent that the string coupling is parametrically small and the internal volume is large in a regime where
\Omega\gg 1
. However, this cannot be combined with scale separation, as the KK scale is of the same order as the AdS scale.

What about higher-derivative corrections? Since the coupling of open strings to the closed string sector in the DBI action appears as a finite
\alpha^{\prime}
effect, one might be worried about higher-order corrections. The NLO corrections in the DBI contributions to the scalar potential come at
\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{4})
, and are of degree four in the fluxes. A typical term of such a form is

g_{1}^{4}\mathrm{Tr}\left(t_{I}t_{I}\right)\mathcal{A}^{8}\rho^{-4}\tau^{-3}\sigma^{-5}\ \sim\ \Omega^{-18}\ ,

(154)

which is indeed subleading w.r.t. the
\Omega^{-14}
scaling of the LO potential terms as read off from table
13
. We checked that the complete set of corrections to the DBI potential at this order only contains terms that scale as
\Omega^{-18}
.

When it comes to higher-derivative corrections to the bulk action, we do not expect anything before
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{\prime 3})
, and these contain,
e.g.
the
\mathcal{R}_{10}^{(4)}
term. A correction to the potential arising from this higher-curvature contribution would scale like

\sqrt{g_{6}}\,\rho^{3}\tau^{-4}\left(\rho^{-1}\mathcal{R}_{6}\right)^{4}e^{-2\Phi}\ \sim\ \Omega^{-20}\ ,

(155)

which is even more suppressed in the large
\Omega
regime. Finally, string loop corrections will be suppressed by extra powers of
g_{s}\,\sim\,\Omega^{-3}
.

Two general comments are due before concluding. The former one concerns the stability issue for the non-supersymmetric AdS vacua presented here. Though they appear perturbatively stable, one might expect there to be a non-perturbative mechanism that destabilizes these solutions, in line with the AdS swampland conjecture stated in
[
40
]
. However, the concrete construction of a viable decay channel still remains to be investigated.

The latter issue is to do with our way of understanding the AdS/CFT correspondence, even in a supersymmetric setup. Generically, we think of it as a holographic duality between the closed string setup that engineers a supergravity AdS vacuum and the corresponding strongly coupled CFT describing the open strings living on the worldvolume of the branes. In our case, the very AdS vacuum relies on a (semiclassical) coupling between closed strings and the open strings living on the worldvolume of the spacetime filling sources supporting the solution. This extra complication would require a rethinking of the holographic correspondence in presence of such brane sources. At present, we cannot yet argue that these vacua admit a CFT dual.

Appendix A
A democratic formulation for massive IIA supergravity

Type IIA supergravity with Roman’s mass
[
41
]
admits a democratic formulation
[
31
]
. The bosonic sector thereof contains the usual NS-NS fields
\{G,B_{(2)},\Phi\}
, whereas the R-R degrees of freedom are doubled
\{C_{2p+1}\}_{p=0,1,2,3,4}
. The following pseudoaction can be used as a mnemonic to obtain the equations of motion in the boson part:

S=\frac{1}{2\kappa_{10}^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{10}x\ \sqrt{-G}\Bigl(e^{-2\Phi}\bigl(\mathcal{R}+4(\partial\Phi)^{2}-\frac{1}{12}|H_{(3)}|^{2}\bigr)-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{p=0}^{5}\frac{|F_{(2p)}|^{2}}{(2p)!}\Bigr)

(156)

The field strengths appearing in the pseudoaction (
156
) are given by

\displaystyle H_{(3)}=\differential B_{2}\,,\quad H_{(7)}=\differential B_{6}\ +\ \sum_{p=0}^{3}(-1)^{k}C_{(7-2p)}\ \wedge\ \bigr(F_{(2p)}+F_{0}\wedge\frac{B_{(2)}^{p}}{p!}\bigl)\,,

(157)

\displaystyle\quad F=\differential C+\differential B\wedge C+F_{(0)}\,e^{-B}\;.

(158)

Making the formal sums in (
158
) explicit, we can write the field strengths of the R-R fields as

\displaystyle F_{0}=m\quad,

(159)

\displaystyle F_{(2)}=\differential C_{(1)}-F_{(0)}B_{(2)}\quad,

(160)

\displaystyle F_{(4)}=\differential C_{(3)}+H_{(3)}\wedge C_{(1)}+F_{(0)}\frac{B_{(2)}^{2}}{2!}\quad,

(161)

\displaystyle F_{(6)}=\differential C_{(5)}+H_{(3)}\wedge C_{(3)}-F_{(0)}\frac{B_{(2)}^{3}}{3!}\quad,

(162)

\displaystyle F_{(8)}=\differential C_{(7)}+H_{(3)}\wedge C_{(5)}+F_{(0)}\frac{B_{(2)}^{4}}{4!}\quad,

(163)

\displaystyle F_{(10)}=\differential C_{(9)}+H_{(3)}\wedge C_{(7)}-F_{(0)}\frac{B_{(2)}^{5}}{5!}\quad.

(164)

In particular, Roman’s mass contribution is encoded in (
159
),
with constant
m
.

Because of their definitions, the field strengths naturally satisfy the modified Bianchi identities

\displaystyle\differential H_{(3)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential H_{(7)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p=0}^{4}F_{(2p)}\wedge\star F_{(2p+2)}=0\quad,

(165)

\displaystyle\differential F_{(0)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential F_{(2)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(0)}=0\quad,

(166)

\displaystyle\differential F_{(4)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(2)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential F_{(6)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(4)}=0\quad,

(167)

\displaystyle\differential F_{(8)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(6)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential F_{(10)}=0\quad.

(168)

The pseudoaction needs to be supplemented by the following duality relations

F_{(0)}=\star F_{(10)}\;,\qquad F_{(2)}=-\star F_{(8)}\;,\qquad F_{(4)}=\star F_{(6)}\;,\qquad H_{(7)}=e^{-2\Phi}\star H_{(3)}\;.

(169)

This way, the original number of degrees of freedom in the R-R sector is restored. Moreover, (
169
) maps the modified Bianchi identities to the equations of motion for the form fields in the NS-NS and the R-R sector:

\displaystyle\differential\bigl(e^{2\Phi}\star H_{(7)}\bigr)=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\bigl(e^{-2\Phi}\star H_{(3)}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p=0}^{4}F_{(2p)}\wedge\star F_{(2p+2)}=0\quad,

(170)

\displaystyle\differential\star F_{(10)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\star F_{(8)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(10)}=0\quad,

(171)

\displaystyle\differential\star F_{(6)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(8)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\star F_{(4)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(6)}=0\quad,

(172)

\displaystyle\differential\star F_{(2)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(4)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\star F_{(0)}=0\quad.

(173)

such that the R-R sector can be written as

\differential\star F_{(2p)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(2p+2)}=0

(174)

The equations of motions for the dilaton, instead, read

\nabla_{M}\nabla^{M}\Phi-\nabla_{M}\Phi\nabla^{M}\Phi+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{R}-\frac{1}{8\times 3!}|H_{(3)}|^{2}=0\quad,

(175)

whereas those for the metric are

\displaystyle e^{-2\Phi}\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{MN}+2\nabla_{M}\nabla_{N}\Phi-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}H_{MPQ}{H_{N}}^{PQ}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2}(F_{(2)}^{2})_{MN}-\frac{1}{2\times 3!}(F_{(4)}^{2})_{MN}+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}G_{MN}\bigl(|F_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2!}|F_{(2)}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4!}|F_{(4)}|^{2}\bigr)\quad.

(176)

Appendix B
Review of the D
\boldsymbol{p}
-branes effective action (bosonic part)

As already anticipated in (
58
), (
59
), the effective action of a stack of D
6
-branes can be decomposed in two pieces

S^{\textup{DBI}}_{\textrm{D}p}=-T_{\textrm{D}p}\int_{\textup{WV}(\textrm{D}p)}\differential^{p+1}x\;\Tr\bigg( e^{-\hat{\Phi}} \sqrt{-\det(\mathbb{M}_{MN}) \det(\tensor{\mathbb{Q}}{^i_j})} \; \bigg)\quad,

S^{\textup{WZ}}_{\textrm{D}p}=\mu_{\textrm{D}p}\int_{\textup{WV}(\textrm{D}p)}\Tr\{\mathrm{P}\bigg[e^{i\lambda\iota_{Y}\iota_{Y}}\Big(\boldsymbol{\hat{C}}\wedge e^{\hat{B}_{(2)}}\Big)\wedge e^{\lambda\mathcal{F}}\bigg]\bigg\}\quad.

In this appendix we report the key information about these actions, following the notation of
[
30
]
.
The matrices
\mathbb{M}_{MN}
and
\mathchoice{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}
appearing in the DBI action are

\mathbb{M}_{MN}=\mathrm{P}\Big[\hat{E}_{MN}+\hat{E}_{Mi}(\mathbb{Q}^{-1}-\delta)^{ij}\hat{E}_{jN}\Big]+\lambda\mathcal{F}_{MN}\quad,

(177)

\mathchoice{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}=\mathchoice{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}+i\lambda[Y^{i},Y^{k}]\hat{E}_{kj}\quad,

(178)

where

\hat{E}_{\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}}=\hat{g}_{\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}}+\hat{B}_{\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}}\quad.

(179)

In the expression of the WZ term,
\boldsymbol{C}
is meant to be a poliform containing the sum of all the
C_{(p)}
fields,
\iota_{Y}
stands for the interior product by a vector
Y^{i}
and
\mathcal{F}
is the field-strength of the gauge field
\mathcal{A}
living on the brane

\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{d}\mathcal{A}+i\mathcal{A}\wedge\mathcal{A}\;.

(180)

In the above formulas the convention for the 10-dimensional coordinates
x^{\mathcal{M}}
is that they are split in worldvolume coordinates
x^{M}
(which include spacetime coordinates
x^{\mu}
and internal coordinates
x^{a}
) and transverse coordinates
y^{i}
. The position of the non-Abelian
D6
-branes is then encoded in
y^{i}=\lambda Y^{Ii}t_{I}
, with

[t_{I},t_{J}]=-i{g_{IJ}}^{K}t_{K}\quad,\quad\mathrm{Tr}[t_{I},t_{J}]=\delta_{IJ}\quad.

(181)

The symbol
\mathrm{P}[\ldots]
denotes the pullback of the bulk fields over the worldvolume of the
D6
-branes. As an example, the pullback of
\hat{E}_{MN}
is

\mathrm{P}[\hat{E}_{MN}]=\hat{E}_{MN}+\lambda D_{M}Y^{i}\hat{E}_{iN}+\lambda D_{N}Y^{i}\hat{E}_{Mi}+\lambda^{2}D_{M}Y^{i}D_{N}Y^{j}\hat{E}_{ij}\;.

(182)

Here the covariant derivative is defined as

D_{M}Y^{i}\equiv\partial_{M}Y^{i}-i[\mathcal{A}_{M},Y^{i}]\;.

(183)

All the hatted fields are meant to be the fields evaluated at the position of the
D6
-branes with a Taylor expansion around
y^{i}=0
:

\hat{\Phi}(x^{M},y^{i})=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}Y^{i_{1}}\cdots Y^{i_{k}}\partial_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial_{i_{k}}\Phi(x^{M},y^{i})\Big|_{y^{i}=0}\;.

(184)
